Parlay board

How the board is built These are the most confident moneylines left after Pixel’s Pick takes its legs, stacked in confidence order until the combined price reaches +100 or better. No game appears twice, so each ticket is genuinely different rather than a reshuffle of the same games. Short favourites are worth little on their own; combining them is what turns them into a payout worth the risk. It does not turn them into an edge — every leg still carries its own vig, and the tracker records how these actually land.

Full breakdown

Why this is a Pick

Liberty Flames is a Pick: the calibrated chance of winning is 78%. The price (-425) implies 81%, essentially the model's number: a fair price, no edge either way.

Since 2023, college moneylines the model rated as Picks have gone 378-122 (76%), returning -6.2% per unit staked — a solid play at a price that already knows it.

Locked (). This is the pick of record: it will not change and it is what the tracker grades.

How the model sees it playing out

The power ratings see a meaningful but not overwhelming edge for Liberty Flames, 6.8 points of separation before adjustments. Overall, Liberty Flames is the 76th-rated team in the league (average) and New Mexico State Aggies is 124th (a real weakness). Liberty Flames then adds the home-field bump the model fits at 4.9 points. Rolling that together, the engine projects Liberty Flames by 14.5 and gives them a 81% chance to win outright.

Projected score: Liberty Flames 32, New Mexico State Aggies 17 against a market total of 48.5.

On how it plays out: the passing game tilts toward Liberty Flames — a slight edge through the air; and Liberty Flames has the better ground matchup, a slight edge on the run. Expect the favorite to be able to play from ahead and lean on the run late.

The market has this at Liberty Flames -10.5. That is a real disagreement: the model wants 4.1 more points on Liberty Flames than the market is pricing, which is where the value on this game sits.

One caution: the projection sits near 14, a key number in football margins. Games cluster on it, so small errors in the estimate flip outcomes against the spread more often than the point difference suggests.
